This email is being sent to you because either (a) you filed a document with the Office of the
Chief Clerk that made you part of the official mailing list for the above referenced matter, or
(b) notice to you is legally required. As authorized by Texas Water Code 5.128, this electronic
transmittal is replacing the previous practice of hard copy distribution. Amendments to Texas
Government Code 552.137 prompted a change to the agency's privacy policy regarding
confidentiality of certain email addresses. The revised privacy policy can be viewed at
http://www.tceq.state.tx.us/help/policies/electronic_info_policy.html.
Questions regarding this email may be submitted either by replying directly to this email or by
calling Mr. Rhyan Stone with the Air Permits Division at (512) 239-1293.

Dear Senator Middleton:
This letter notifies you that the Texas Commission on Environmental Quality has received a federal
operating permit (FOP) renewal application for a site located in your district. As part of this permitting
process, the applicant is required to publish a formal newspaper public notice. The notice will inform the
public of their right to make comments or request a public hearing. This link to an electronic map of the
site or facility's general location is provided as a public courtesy and not part of the application or notice.
For exact location, refer to application. https://gisweb.tceq.texas.gov/LocationMapper/?marker=-
94.913055,29.353611&level=13.
The FOP program regulates both new and existing major sources of emissions. The goal of the program
is to improve air quality in Texas through increased compliance by codifying existing applicable regulatory
requirements into the FOP. The FOP provides the applicant authorization to operate the equipment at the
site. The FOP identifies and codifies air emission requirements (known as applicable requirements) that
apply to the emission units at the site. The FOP does not authorize construction of emission units or
emissions from those units. The New Source Review (NSR) permit is the mechanism for these
authorizations.
P.O. Box 13087 • Austin, Texas 78711-3087 • 512-239-1000 • tceq.texas.gov

Copyright 1994-2025Process Descrip(cid:415)on
Gulf Coast Authority
40-Acre Wastewater Treatment Facility
Process Descrip(cid:415)on – Wastewater Opera(cid:415)ons
The Gulf Coast Authority’s (GCA) 40-Acre biological wastewater treatment facility is
located in Texas City, Texas. Construc(cid:415)on of the current treatment facility started in 1972. The
facility began opera(cid:415)ng in 1974.
Major units in the wastewater treatment system are: an oxygen ac(cid:415)vated sludge unit,
two clarifiers, a digester, two basins designated LAB1 and LAB2 (historical names retained for
facility iden(cid:415)fica(cid:415)on; currently operated as faculta(cid:415)ve/polishing basins), an anaerobic basin,
two stabiliza(cid:415)on basins (STAB1 and STAB2), and two faculta(cid:415)ve basins (FAC2 and FAC3).
Suppor(cid:415)ng facili(cid:415)es include two wastewater spli(cid:425)er boxes, a process wastewater sump, and
weir systems.
The flow diagram shows the general rou(cid:415)ng of wastewater through the 40-Acre Facility
treatment process. The 40-Acre Facility provides wastewater treatment for four industrial users
– three chemical manufacturing plants (Union Carbide Corpora(cid:415)on A Subsidiary of the Dow
Chemical Company Texas City, UCC/Dow), INEOS Styrolu(cid:415)on America, LLC (INEOS), and INEOS
Acetyls, formally Eastman Chemical Texas City (ECTC)) and a marine terminal opera(cid:415)on Advario,
Texas City L.P formally (Oiltanking Texas City, OT). The names of the current industrial discharges
to the 40-Acre Facility are provided for consistency with opera(cid:415)onal nomenclature. The names
of the industrial dischargers are not intended as a permit representa(cid:415)on.
Several other much smaller waste streams are also treated at the 40-Acre Facility; these
are storm water and carriage water from the 40-Acre landfarm, which receives the sludge from
the wastewater treatment plant, and storm water and non-hazardous leachate from GCA’s
Campbell Bayou Facility (CBF). Sanitary sewage generated at the 40-Acre Facility from its office
buildings is treated in a sanitary water treatment package plant; a(cid:332)er disinfec(cid:415)on with chlorine
tablets, the effluent is routed to the Limited Aera(cid:415)on Basin #1 (LAB1). Sanitary sewage from the
maintenance building is collected in the sep(cid:415)c tank and the tank effluent is treated in the
oxygen ac(cid:415)vated sludge unit (OASU).
Pretreated wastewater from UCC/Dow, INEOS Styrolu(cid:415)on, and Advario is piped directly
to the OASU where it is treated biologically. Oxygen to support the biological process is supplied
by pure oxygen (as opposed to air). If necessary, any approved wastewater streams may also be
delivered to the facility by truck and enter the wastewater treatment system at the OASU. Other
Process Descrip(cid:415)on August 2026streams that may also enter at this point are sanitary wastewater from INEOS Acetyls and any
seed biosludge that may be needed for process control.
The ac(cid:415)vated sludge mixed liquor (biological solids) from the OASU is routed via an
overflow box to two secondary clarifiers (CLAR1, CLAR2). The treated wastewater from the
clarifiers is routed to a flow-through sump known as the process water tank (PWT). Pumps in
the PWT draw small volumes of wastewater for sampling and washdown ac(cid:415)vi(cid:415)es, as needed.
Most of the wastewater flows through the PWT to a downstream valve that splits the flow to
the limited aera(cid:415)on basins (LAB1, LAB2), which provide addi(cid:415)onal biological treatment.
Wastewater passes through a former spli(cid:425)er box (INFBOX) enroute to LAB2. Thickened
biological solids (return ac(cid:415)vated sludge) from the clarifiers are returned to the OASU, and a
small frac(cid:415)on of the return ac(cid:415)vated sludge is wasted to the digester. The 40-Acre Facility also
uses a portable electric dredge to periodically remove solids from the treatment basins. ANA2
collects direct rainfall and may be pumped to the digester, as needed, to maintain pond water
levels.
Wastewater from LAB1, LAB2, the digester, INEOS Acetyls, and addi(cid:415)onal UCC/Dow flow
enters the treatment system via a second spli(cid:425)er box (SPLITBOX) from where it flows to the first
stabiliza(cid:415)on basin (STAB1), which provides biological treatment. The addi(cid:415)onal waste stream
from UCC/DOW consists of clarified solids from treatment of its raw water supply: the waste
stream contains no vola(cid:415)le organic chemicals. Wastewater from STAB1 flows to the second
stabiliza(cid:415)on basin (STAB2), then to the two faculta(cid:415)ve basins (FAC2, FAC3). Treated wastewater
effluent from FAC2 and FAC3 is discharged from the 40-Acre Facility through the 40-Acre
discharge line via gravity flow and is controlled by a discharge control valve.
Other emission units at the 40-Acre Facility are a gasoline storage tank (GASTANK3),
which provides fuel for vehicles used on-site; fugi(cid:415)ve emissions associated with gasoline storage
and dispensing equipment (FUG1); storage tank for inorganic 25-50% sodium hydroxide
(CAUSTIC1); and portable equipment with diesel and gasoline engines.

(cid:9)Attachment #1, OP-ACPS
Part 2 – Items 4 and 5
40-Acre Facility – Title V Permit Renewal
Explanation of Deviation and Corrective Actions
Non-Compliance Situation Description
The reported deviation from Special Condition 3 (SC 3) resulted from a temporary failure
of the Oxygen Activated Sludge Unit (OASU) impoundment liner. The liner failure
required the OASU to be removed from service and wastewater to be temporarily diverted
while the facility implemented corrective actions and evaluated the appropriate permitting
path.
The OASU is not currently operating under normal service conditions. The temporary
diversion was implemented as an interim operating measure while the facility addresses
the liner failure and associated permitting requirements.
Corrective Actions
Corrective actions include:
• Investigating the root cause of the OASU impoundment liner failure.
• Repairing or replacing the failed liner and restoring the OASU to service following
completion of repairs.
• Implementing appropriate corrective measures to prevent recurrence of a similar
liner failure.
• Completing any necessary permitting modifications, if it is determined that
emissions associated with the temporary operating configuration exceed the
applicable Maximum Allowable Emission Rates (MAERTs).
Current Status
The OASU remains out of service pending completion of liner repairs. Wastewater is being
managed under the temporary operating configuration while the facility completes
corrective actions and continues evaluating permitting requirements associated with the
temporary operation.
